# PHASE4_IMPL — construction record for Phase 4 (`engine/iop` + sif + cdvd + STREAM.IRX + IOP-bus devices)

Plan: `PHASE4_PLAN.md` (§7 stages, §6 gate, §6.1 claim table). Rules: `STAGE_RULES.md`. One section per stage; every
stage ends with the tree building (`cmake --build --preset macos-dev -j4`) and `ctest --preset macos-dev` green and a
HANDOFF in the §8.5 form. Findings go to the plan's §7.1 ledger as `P4-<NAME>` rows (mirrored here).

### Findings (→ plan §7.1 rows)

| Id | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| P4-CEILING-INCLUSIVE | OLD `IopModuleTable::DrainQuantum` breaks only when the next due is PAST the ceiling (`> ceiling`), so a due that falls exactly on the next boundary's tick is serviced one pump early: N pumps from t=0 fire `floor((N+1)·16667/period)` heartbeat beats — 61 for the 60 Hz timer over 60 pumps, one more than the plan's clock-7 phrase "N / HeartbeatTicksFor". Same code in OLD; the paired `iopsched` census would have caught a change. | Transplanted verbatim (the test pins the inclusive rule and prints both numbers); the §4.3 clock-7 oracle phrase reads "over N pumps with the inclusive ceiling". `DrainQuantum` itself lands with `IopModuleTable` in 4A′ with this exact comparison and its hot-loop guard. |
| P4-NO-FIBER-LAYER | The plan's "tree's platform fiber layer (the EE scheduler's)" does not exist in NEW (Phase-1 stubs; `has_fibers=false`). | Added `FiberMacos` (ucontext) behind the Platform entries + two new entries (`fiber_current`, `fiber_destroy`); no `#ifdef` outside `engine/platform/`. |
| P4-GUARD-HOOKS-DROPPED | OLD's `SetDispatchGuard`/`SetContextSwitchHooks` exist only for the deleted `GuestMonitor`; §2.1 said "installed EMPTY". | Not written (fewer moving parts); `SetThreadRunHooks`/`SetWakeHook`/`SetTraceHook` kept. |

- `tests/iop/test_fiber_guard.cpp` → **`FiberGuardPage`**: 5 checks PASS (probe 1 `sig=10`, probe 2 `sig=10`, probe 3 exit 0).
- `tests/iop/test_module_table.cpp` → **`IopModuleTable`**: 35 checks PASS — quantum drain (after every boundary pump the next due is past the inclusive ceiling and within one period of it; time never runs past the ceiling; 15..18 beats of a 1 ms timer per pump; recorded 134 beats over 8 pumps — one beat per pass + forward resync at the frame jump, the kernel's no-burst rule), inclusive ceiling (**61 beats over 60 pumps** of the 16,667 µs timer — P4-CEILING-INCLUSIVE), park-loop pump bound (≤ one quantum per call; a 100 ms delay resumes after 7 pumps at t=100,000), `OnModuleLoaded` runs every started thread to its first block (priority order, `UnparkedCount()==0`), no-op rules (before boot; under REALTIME `Frames()` stays 0, host thread running, `Shutdown` stops it), `PumpAfterEeService` resumes exactly the woken `Wait::Rpc` parkee (a Sleep parkee untouched, no frame advance), policy from `Config` (frame/realtime; shipped default Frame), module census `sio2man:515,padman:516,stream:0`, watch-line grammar `[IOPK-WATCH] t1=3/1 t2=0/0 t3=0/0 t4=0/0 unparked=0`.
- `tests/iop/test_sif_rpc_bridge.cpp` → **`SifRpcBridge`**: 23 checks PASS — memcpy + wakeup (send buffer copied into the server buffer ON tid 1 = the `RpcServiceLoop` thread, ticket completed under FRAME, second command via the banked wakeup, 0 stalls), reply copy-back at the slot (worker parked in `Wait::Rpc` survives a boundary, then copy-back + `NotifyEeReply` + `PumpAfterEeService` → resumed with `woken=true` and the reply bytes visible), drop-late-reply (1 ms deadline expires at the boundary pump → `woken=false` → the worker's next Sleep is NOT released by the late `NotifyEeReply`, nor by `iWakeupIfRpcWait`; a real `iWakeupThread` does — control), inline on kernel thread (`Submitted()==0`), REALTIME host thread completes a command (`Pokes()>=1`, `Passes()>=1`, `Shutdown` joins).

### Findings (→ plan §7.1 rows)

| Id | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| P4-HOOK-AFTER-HOST | `IopModuleTable`'s members die in reverse order (host thread before kernel); `~IopKernel` → `Shutdown()` → the wake hook → `IopHostThread::Notify()` on a destroyed mutex (`system_error: mutex lock failed`, seen in both new tests' teardown under REALTIME). OLD never destroys its `static` instance, so it never saw this. | `IopModuleTable::Shutdown()` joins the host thread and then detaches `SetWakeHook`/`SetHostWake`. |
| P4-TIMER-NO-BURST | A sub-frame periodic timer (1 ms) under the boundary pump does NOT fire `floor((N+1)·16667/period)` beats: the kernel fires one beat per pass and resyncs forward at the frame jump (4A's "no beat burst"), so 8 pumps give 134 beats, not 150. The clock-7 phrase holds for periods ≥ one quantum (the title's heartbeat range `[1000, 100000]` µs includes 1 ms — a 36.864 MHz compare of `0x9000`). | Pinned as the contract (next due past the inclusive ceiling after every pump, time never past the ceiling, 15..18 per pump) + the recorded 134; the paired `iopsched` census remains the leg-side oracle. |
| P4-MAP-ANON | `_XOPEN_SOURCE 700` (needed for ucontext) hides both `MAP_ANONYMOUS` and `MAP_ANON` on macOS. | `_DARWIN_C_SOURCE` defined in the same TU. |

Root cause: `IopHostThread::Loop` counted a pass AFTER `kernel_.Dispatch()` returned while the RPC ticket that releases the EE submitter completes INSIDE `Dispatch()`, so the EE side could observe a completed command with the census still at 0. The 4B gate line therefore stands as "83 tests, SifRpcBridge flaky" — fixed at root and re-proved in 4B′ (see below). ## 4B′ — race fix, ABI root, stub surface, STREAM.IRX link, loader, kernel-facing modules (2026-09-27, ≈35 min)

### Blocking race (first 8 minutes)
- `engine/iop/iop_host_thread.cpp`: `passes_` is now incremented BEFORE `kernel_.Dispatch()` (the pass is counted on kernel ENTRY). An EE submitter is released by its RPC ticket from inside `Dispatch()`, so the after-the-fact increment let the EE-side observer see a completed command with `Passes()` still 0. Under FRAME the host thread is never started, so the `IopModuleTable` assertions `Passes() == 0` (test_module_table.cpp:133/135) stay valid and green.
- Proof (bounded, `/tmp/p4b2/rerun_bridge.sh`, `verify/run_bounded.sh 30` per run): **25/25 passed** on the first rebuilt binary, then **20/20 passed** on the final tree (45/45 total, `ALL PASS: 0 failure(s)` each). Full `ctest --preset macos-dev` twice: 83/83 (before the new tests) and 86/86 (final).
- The 4B gate line above carries the correction; no test was weakened — `Pokes() >= 1 && Passes() >= 1` remains the assertion.

Kernel context is unchanged (cooperative `DelayUs` park, 100 ms clamp).

### Tests added (ctest names) — result
- **`StreamIrxLoadsAndParks`** (OLD `game_files/STREAM.IRX` read-only, 116 156 bytes; SKIP when absent): `sections: text 76744 data 1272 rodata 616 bss 55360; ok`; entry ran; table booted; 30 module rows; `UnroutedCount() == 0`. **First-block quote:** `first block: rpc tid 1 ([0x13328] = 1); tid 1 Wait/Sleep prio 30 (rpc); kernel: unparked 0; timers: hard timer 1 idle; libsd calls: init 0 setparam 0; cd init 0` — the entry creates and starts ONE thread (tid 1, priority 30, the module's main thread), stores its tid at `[0x13328]`, and that thread runs its init prologue and parks in **`SleepThread` (Wait::Sleep)** before `SdInit`, the hard-timer heartbeat and `sceSifRpcLoop`. Those follow the EE's wakeup (the game's `sceSifBindRpc`/`CallRpc`, Phase 4C) — so at this park the heartbeat is NOT yet armed and no device call has happened (asserted). The plan's expectation "RPC thread parked in `RpcServiceLoop`, heartbeat armed" describes the park AFTER the EE handshake, not the boot-path first block; the test pins what the boot path actually does (finding P4-FIRST-BLOCK below).
- **`IopmBootSymmetricRepark`** (same binary, mode `repark`): snapshot the boot park (32 thread states/wait kinds/registered, 64 sema active/counts) → `IopmSection::MakeSpec(plugin.IopmDeps(...)).serialize` (payload = `kPayloadBytes`) → perturb (`RestoreHeapPtr(+0x1000)`, one `SdVoiceTrans` → pending seq 1) → `spec.restore` (Parse + `ReconcileKernel` + `after_restore` = `BootSymmetricRepark`) → **`re-park: tid 1 Wait/Sleep prio 30 (rpc)`**, all thread/sema fields equal the boot park, `UnparkedCount() == 0`, pending seq 0, `Heap().HeapPtr() == statics.heap_ptr`. ALL PASS.
- `StreamIrxStubSurface` + `TestLinkGuard` (248 / 0x13C / 0x1930).

**Done:** link guard real (+ negative proof), census 74/72 recorded, DelayThread deviation noted; `module_layout.h`, `feed_contract.h`, `LibsdModule` (16), `CdvdmanModule` (9), `IdleModule` (6 rows), `LtiProtocol` (0x12345 + CD-read body per OLD :470-540), `StreamIrxPlugin` (bind/resolve/boot/rows/IOPM deps/BootSymmetricRepark), `nativeee` links `ps2r_title_stream_irx`; tests `StreamIrxLoadsAndParks` + `IopmBootSymmetricRepark`; ctest 88/88, lints green.
**Not done / carried:** (1) `feed_contract.h`'s `kTransIntrHandlerOffset` firing (the sound core fires it after VoiceTrans; 4E) — LibsdModule only records the handler. (2) The IOPM re-park oracle ran against a self-produced section; the OLD anchor `.p2s` IOPM sections (`test_iopm_section` reads them as a layout oracle) were not fed through `BootSymmetricRepark` — do it in 4C's savestate leg once the EE side exists (an anchor's thread flags include threads the EE handshake started). (3) `PS2_HB_TICKCLOCK` (the heartbeat's clock) is still open: the beat is armed only after the EE wakeup, so it lands with 4C. (4) Plan §7.1 rows P4-LINKGUARD-VACUOUS / P4-FIRST-BLOCK / P4-IDLE-SIX not yet copied into `PHASE4_PLAN.md` (doc edit only). (5) The composition root does not boot the plugin yet (boot order: EE parks at `sceSifInitRpc` until 4C).
**Next command:** `cmake --build --preset macos-dev -j4 && ctest --preset macos-dev` (expect 88/88), then 4C: bind `EeRamSink` to EE RAM and `SifRpcBridge` to the EE's `sceSifBindRpc(0x12345)`, boot `StreamIrxPlugin` from the composition root after the EE reaches `sceSifInitRpc`, and write `StreamIrxServesFirstRpc` = the EE wakeup drives tid 1 out of `SleepThread` through SdInit → `StartHardTimer` (heartbeat armed, `Timrman().Running(1)`) → `sceSifRpcLoop` (Wait::Rpc), then the first `main_handler` (0x8D28) call returns through the bridge.

### Decisions recorded (brief items 8)
- **End-callback context (`RpcRouter::CompletePending`)**: the live-context form stays, with the invariant stated in `rpc_router.h`: a boundary is reached only from an HLE call site (GsSync / a syscall face) where the generated caller has already spilled every register it keeps live across the call per the MIPS ABI, so a callback's clobber of the other caller-saved registers is unobservable to the interrupted guest; a boundary from inside a generated function would require the full caller-saved set. `EeRpcServer::Service` — which runs a *server function* (not an ABI-visible callback) from slot 6 AND from the bridge's poll inside `sceSifCallRpc` — takes the original's borrowed-context form (every GPR + pc saved/restored) because its second entry point is mid-HLE-body where the caller's a0–a3/t0–t3 decode is still live in `ctx`.
- **Dmac IMPL row addendum**: the original's D6 `CHCR.STR` kick ran `ProcessSifDma` (the SIF command-packet interpreter, `dmac.cpp:137-230`); NEW completes the channel with no interpretation. Leg invariant **`dmac_sif_kicks==0`** — held on both legs this stage (0 / 0); a non-zero value on any leg is a park-class finding, not a tolerance.

### Tests added (ctest names) — result
- **`SifFlagSingleSource`** (`tests/sif/test_sif_flag_source.cpp`): guest `sceSifSetReg(SMFLAG, 1)` → `SifState::Smflag()==1`; `SceSif::InitRpc` → `SetSmflagBits(0x60000)` → **`sceSifGetReg(SMFLAG) == 0x60001`** (the IOP-side bits visible after the guest SetReg; a plain `memory::read(0x1000F230)` agrees; the CMDINIT poll succeeds at once); `sceSifSetReg(MSFLAG, 0x30000)` → SMFLAG `0x70001` via the shared word; a later guest store replaces the word for both faces; an IOP-side raise after it is visible again; MSCOM round trip; indices 0/7 read 0; system registers keep their own words; `UnhandledPageCount()==0`. ALL PASS.
- **`SceSifDma`** (`tests/sif/test_sce_sif_dma.cpp`): EE → IOP through `SyscallTable::SifSetDma` (syscall 119) + the root's byte copy into `IopMemory` over `{src,dest,size,attr}` × 4 (aligned block; odd src/dest/size with attr 4; zero size; a 0x101-byte entry crossing an IOP 64 KB boundary) — byte for byte with guard bytes on both sides of every destination; return rule: command list → id 1, a list without attr&4 → 0 (bytes still move), count 0 → 0, next command lists → 2, 3 (attr 0x7 carries the bit); `SifDmaBytes()` census exact (412 B). IOP → EE through `MainMemoryEeRamSink::WriteEeRam`: 0x233 B at a physical address byte for byte (visible through `memory::read`), KSEG0 `0x80401001` and KSEG1 `0xA0401010` masked to physical, a range past 32 MB and an address at the end refused (`false`, census counts only accepted writes: 571 B). Quoted: `sif dma: ee->iop 412 bytes (ids 1,0,0,2,3); iop->ee 571 bytes`. ALL PASS.
- **`EeRpcServer`** (`tests/sif/test_ee_rpc_server.cpp`, FRAME, `IopModuleTable` + `IopMemory` + test EE RAM + `SceSif::Bind`): (a) before any `sceSifRegisterRpc` a worker's `RequestAndWait` returns false at once (`Rejected()==1`, never parks); (b) `SceSif::RegisterRpc(sid 0x1234, func, buff)` → a worker's `RequestAndWait(sid, fno 7, 0x38 B, 0x2C B)`: request bytes in `buff` byte for byte (guard intact), worker in `Wait::Rpc` (`EeWaitTid()==3`), a boundary without service leaves it parked; `Service(ctx)` with every GPR stamped → the guest call saw **a0 = 7, a1 = buff, a2 = 0x38, ra = 0x80001000**, clobbered all 31 GPRs + pc → **every GPR (both halves) and the pc restored**; `recv_size` reply bytes in the IOP recv buffer byte for byte (guard intact); `Served()==1`; the wake lands at the slot-6 pump (`PumpAfterEeService` → `RequestAndWait` returned true, worker on to its next park, `EeWaitTid()==0`); a second `Service` serves nothing; (c) sid mismatch → `Rejected()==2`; (d) 0x200/0x200 sizes → **0x100 clamp** both ways (byte 0xFF copied, 0x100 untouched, a2 = 0x100), `Served()==2`; (e) deadline: the request withdrawn after **7 frame boundaries** (`Timeouts()==1`, `RequestAndWait` false, `Pending()` false), a late `Service` finds nothing (`seen.calls` unchanged), a late `NotifyEeReply` is dropped (the worker's Sleep not released); `UnroutedCount()==0`. Quoted: `round trip: fno 7 buff 00310000 size 56 ra 80001000; served 1 rejected 1 timeouts 0` / `deadline: expired after 7 boundaries; served 2 rejected 2 timeouts 1`. ALL PASS. Rig note: later-started workers run to their first block through `IopKernel::Dispatch()` (the table boots once — `OnModuleLoaded` is a no-op after that).
- **`StreamIrxServesFirstRpc`** second case — **the init command quote**: request `{header 0, count 1, cmd 12, nparams 4, config 1, p1 0, sectors 0x10, flags 0}` (16 B) through `SceSif::CallRpc` on the same client → `init command (cmd 12): face calls 2 stalls 0; bridge submitted 2 completed 2` / **`tid 1: Wait/Sleep; libsd init 0 setparam 0; hard timer 1 RUNNING; unparked 0; protocol calls 0 bridged 0`** / `libsd ordinals after cmd 12: 0 0 0 2 2 0 48 0 0 0 0 1 0 0 0 1` / `libsd ordinals after 8 boundaries: 0 0 384 18 18 0 432 0 0 0 0 9 0 0 0 1; tid 1 Wait/Sleep; hard timer 1 RUNNING; unparked 0`. Asserted: the heartbeat is ARMED by cmd 12 (`Timrman().Running(1)`), the sound core is set up through libsd **SdSetSwitch ×2 / SdGetSwitch ×2 / SdGetAddr ×48** (+ ordinals 11, 15), **libsd ordinal 0 (`SdInit`) is never called** — the brief's `Libsd().Calls(0)==1` expectation was wrong about the real module and is pinned the other way (P4-CDINIT-SOUND-SHAPE); over the next 8 boundaries the armed heartbeat polls the core (SdGetParam ×384, SdGetAddr → 432) with tid 1 parked in the service loop; the init reply is `kRpcResultBuffer` byte for byte; the zeroed-request case stays as the "not the init command" pin. ALL PASS (bounded, `/tmp/p4c4/init3.log`).

### First 10 minutes — BUG-040 (`docs/bugs/BUG-040-ee-run-token-test-hang.md`, status FIXED)
| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `engine/ee/sched/ee_run_token.{h,cpp}` | **Engine fix.** `GuestExit` RETIRES the tid (`retired_[kMaxTid=256]`); `MakeReady` of a retired tid with no slot is a counted no-op (`RetiredReadyNoops()`), so a READY slot no host thread will ever attach cannot exist and cannot be elected (the wedge: `owner_` = an exited tid, every later `GuestEnter` waits for `owner_ == kNone`); the next `GuestEnter` of that tid attaches and un-retires it (StartThread of a reused thread id spawns a fresh host thread). `ResetForTest` clears both. The HostGate pseudo-tid (−2) is never retired. |
| `tests/ee/test_ee_sched.cpp` | **Rig fix**: `boss`/`lo` publish `boss_holds`/`lo_holds` after their `GuestEnter`; workers are launched only after it and count themselves in BEFORE their `GuestEnter` (`TestPriorityElection`, `TestPreemption`, `TestChangePriorityTail`; order assertions untouched). **Regression** `TestRetiredTidCannotWedge` — `b040_makeready_of_exited_tid_is_counted_noop`, `b040_exited_tid_never_elected`, `b040_next_guest_enter_runs_after_exited_tid_made_ready` (5 s deadline, a wedge is reported through `Shutdown()`, never a hang), `b040_reused_tid_reattaches_on_guest_enter`. |

**Proof.** With the retired check disabled (temporary `false &&`, reverted; `/tmp/p4d/nofix_run.log`): `FAIL b040_makeready_of_exited_tid_is_counted_noop / FAIL b040_exited_tid_never_elected / FAIL b040_next_guest_enter_runs_after_exited_tid_made_ready`, `FAILURES: 3` — the wedge reproduces deterministically (tid 72 parked until the deadline). With the fix (`/tmp/p4d/fix_run2.log`): `ALL PASS`. **Repeat** (`/tmp/p4d/flake10.log`, `ctest -R '^EeRunTokenAndEventQueue$' --repeat until-fail:10 --timeout 120`, bounded, load avg 5.73/6.07/6.62 — the load class that produced all 4 earlier timeouts): **10/10 Passed (0.06–0.27 s), `100% tests passed, 0 tests failed out of 1`, 0.86 s.** Known cost recorded in BUG-040: a REUSED tid's StartThread pre-enqueue is now the no-op (its FIFO seq is assigned at host arrival) — closing it needs a spawn signal from `ThreadManager::StartThread` (Phase-3 file, D-SEQ-2 note) → 4D′/4E. In-game reachability: every `MakeReady` site is status-guarded; the counted no-op is the diagnostic.

The four libdma rows (`memclr`, `sceDmaGetChan`, `sceDmaReset`, `sceDmaPutEnv`) are Phase-5 (`dmac/hle/libdma_bindings.cpp`); OLD's bodies (`sce_hle/libdma.cpp`, 82 ln): `sceDmaReset`/`sceDmaPutEnv` → 0 no-ops, `memclr` = zero fill, `sceDmaGetChan` = a per-channel 0x40-byte descriptor block at a host-invented EE address `0x01CFF000` (an OLD quirk: the real sceDmaChan table is libdma's own static data in the title's .data). | **Decision for the plan owner (4D″ opener):** either pull the four libdma rows forward as a Phase-4 trap release (a small `engine/dmac/hle/libdma_bindings.cpp`, real bodies over the Phase-4C `Dmac` register page — `sceDmaReset` writes D_CTRL/D_STAT/D_PCR... per the SDK; `sceDmaGetChan` returns the title's static table address, NOT `0x01CFF000`), or leave them to Phase 5 and accept that the coldboot leg's SIF traffic quote waits for it. Not done here (scope rule). |
| P4-STSTAT-ADDR-MISLABEL | OLD hooked `0x2c4578/0x2c4610/0x2c46c8` as `sceCdStStat/Pause/Resume` (`cdvd_stream.h`, "decompilation pass4"); the title's `sdk_bindings.tsv` (the SDK 3.0.1 signature match) names them `sceCdGetError/sceCdBreak/sceCdMmode`. OLD therefore returned the always-full-ring value (16 / ring) from `sceCdGetError` while a stream was active (0 = SCECdErNO when idle) — Break/Mmode agree (1) either way. | The tsv names stand (`sceCdGetError` → 0 always). The always-full-ring rule lives in `StreamReader::Stat`, reached through `sceCdStream` cmd 6. If a leg's `mem.mhash`/control flow moves on `sceCdGetError` while streaming, this is the EQUIVALENCE §7 record to write. |
| P4-RTC-LAYOUT | `sceScfGetLocalTimefromRTC` writes OLD's six binary bytes (not the SDK's in-place BCD conversion). With `sceCdReadClock` now writing the BCD sceCdCLOCK first, the same 8-byte struct ends `00 00 0c 0f 06 05 06 05` (bytes 6-7 = the BCD month/year from ReadClock; OLD left them 0). | Accepted-difference candidate with P4-CDCLOCK-RTC-VS-ZERO (one EQUIVALENCE §7 record when a leg's `mem.mhash` moves on it). |
| P4-MMIO-PHASE5-PAGES | The libdev VU/VIF resets write GIF/VIF1 register pages and the VIF1 FIFO (`0x10003000, 0x10003c00, 0x10005000`): `unhandled_mmio=3`. | Phase 5 pages; the sink's idle values stand. Recorded so the gate's `UnhandledRanges()` expectation for Phase 4 legs reads 3 pages, not 0. |

### HANDOFF (4D′ → 4D″)
**Done:** (1) the libscf RTC row (`RtcPark` retired; OLD's six-byte layout over `DerivedClocks::Rtc`); (2) `StreamReader` + the three libcdvd stream rows (`sceCdStInit/sceCdStRead/sceCdStream` dispatcher) + `CdvdStreamReader` test (ring round trip vs the ISO) + `LibcdvdBindings` stream cases; (3) `DeliveryMeter` (Instant / Modelled over `cdvd.latency_model`+`cdvd.latency_scale`) + `DeliveryMeter` test + the slot-4 duty order (CD delivery → RPC end-callbacks) + `cdvd_deliveries=`/`cdvd_latency_model=` run.meta keys; (4) `CdvdmanModule::sceCdRead/sceCdSearchFile` real bodies over the extended `CdvdReader` (`ReadSectors`/`FindFile`), the deferred callback through the kernel (`ScheduleEventUs(now+3000)`, plugin-installed runner) + `CdvdmanModuleDisc` test; (5) `RecompSymbolCensus` TIMEOUT 300, `iso_fs.h` comment path, `IsoFs` 64-bit offsets, `LibcdvdBindings::Read` block copy via the new `memory::get_pointer`; (6) coldboot + anchor legs re-run and quoted (above).
**Not done / carried:** (a) **the coldboot leg parks on `libdma.a sceDmaReset` (0x2c1f18)** — P4-LIBDMA-PARK needs the plan owner's call (pull the four libdma rows forward as a small Phase-4 trap release over the 4C `Dmac` register page, or wait for Phase 5); until then `sif_bound=`/`stream_rpc_calls=` stay empty and the cmd-12 libsd shape comparison to P4-CDINIT-SOUND-SHAPE waits; (b) no producer calls `DeliveryMeter::Schedule` yet — the EE-side deferred-read path (`hle/loader_model` + `SetTraceSink` + the oracle checker port) and the `R140Budget` port (`delivery_budget`, 256 KB/tick refill on epoch edges) are 4D″'s; (c) the cdvdman completion is IOP-visible only (kernel event); an EE-visible hop through the meter lands with the loader model; (d) the pre-registered 4D′ list (`latency_model` as its own class — today the model is inside `DeliveryMeter::DueTick`; `core/diag/loadspan`; the two workarounds; `archive_table` + `ArchiveTableLayout`); (e) BUG-040's reused-tid seq window; (f) a title-side `PowerOffCB` row if a leg reaches it; (g) EQUIVALENCE §7 records for P4-RTC-LAYOUT / P4-CDCLOCK-RTC-VS-ZERO / P4-STSTAT-ADDR-MISLABEL when a leg's `mem.mhash` moves on them.
**Next command:** `cmake --build --preset macos-dev -j4 && ctest --preset macos-dev --timeout 300` (expect 100/100), then decide P4-LIBDMA-PARK; with the libdma rows bound re-run `verify/run_leg.py --binary build/app/host/nativeee --tree new --anchor coldboot --profile deterministic --frames 1500 --out /tmp/p4d2/cb` and quote `park=`, `sif_bound=`, `sif_rpc_calls=`, `stream_rpc_calls=` (the first STREAM.IRX command sequence; cmd-12 libsd shape vs P4-CDINIT-SOUND-SHAPE).

(6, 2) → 5; else the state byte. `scePadInfoAct` (`:274`): the DualShock 2 descriptor table `{00 01 01 FF},{01 01 01 FF}`, -1 → 2, term 1..4. `scePadRead` (`:327`): `length` from `+0x60` (cap 0x80), byte copy (OLD's aligned 64-bit fast path copies the same bytes), returns length; static buffer `+0xC..` six bytes, `+4`=1, `+8`=6), `scePadInfoMode` (`:861`: task/runTask gate, the four PAD_MODECUR* cases — NEW's case 4 masks the table index with `& 0xF` (the 16-entry table at `+0x50`), OLD did not; **The R183 press latch — decided (P4-PAD-LATCH-DELETED, plan §7.1):** OLD `pad_input.cpp:226-262` (`ApplyPressLatch`, `PadInput_NoteRead`, `kLatchMinReads = 2`) held a pressed bit in the wire until the game had performed two button-payload reads after the host last saw the key held — a repair for its own ~880 Hz re-stamping from instantaneous host key state against a title that reads the pad once per game frame (OLD's own measurement: 4/4 taps stamped, 0/4 served). NEW stamps ONCE per boundary from a per-frame sample; a script press over `[F1,F2]` is served on exactly those frames and cannot be un-stamped between reads. NORM → not written. Sub-frame host taps are the Phase-7 host sampler's concern (edge-latching where the host is sampled), never the protocol's.

### Findings (→ plan §7.1 rows, written there)
- **P4-PAD-LATCH-DELETED** — the R183 press latch (above).
- **P4-PAD-RELEASE** — both parks released; the sound init precedes `scePadPortOpen`; the anchor's pad image is served from the restored RAM (Phase 6 savestate section).
- **P4-PADMAN-READ-FANOUT (accepted difference, recorded by 4E″ from the p4e2r review)** — OLD `HandlePadman` (`sif_rpc_handlers.cpp:939-961`) writes the 8-byte idle report for opcode 6 (PadRead) and then falls into the common result fan-out (`:1008-1012`), which overwrites reply bytes `+0x00..+0x03` with `01 00 00 00` (the `result = 1` word) — the served report is therefore `01 00 00 00 80 80 80 80`, not `00 70 FF FF 80 80 80 80`. NEW's `PadmanModule::kOpRead` returns after the report (`padman_module.cpp:33-39`) and serves the report intact — the documented PS2 shape (status 0, DualShock mode 0x70, active-low button word `FF FF`). OLD is wrong; the difference is accepted per `EQUIVALENCE_PLAN.md` §7 and unreachable in both legs (`padman_calls=0/0/0/0`: libpad 3.0.1 reads the pad through the slot-4 stamp, not the padman read opcode). Row in plan §7.1.

### Tests added (ctest names) — result
- **`SpuCoreHammer`** (#112, `tests/sound/test_spu_core_hammer.cpp`): a producer thread issuing the libsd surface 20 000× (5 params, SSA/LSAX, KON, NAX/ENVX polls, KOFF every 8th, ENDX clear every 64th, MVOL) against the mixer thread pumping 64-frame blocks, **20 s deadline** (a timeout fails the test instead of hanging it): terminates, `polls == 40000`, every poll in range (NAX < 2 MB, ENVX ≤ 0x7FFF), `KonCount == 20000`, the ring drained, no hold survives when nothing was dropped. `all vectors passed` (`pumps=3 polls=40000 dropped=53196 holds=166` — the overflow path is exercised; holds only from dropped Kons, as designed).
- **`SoundState`** (#113, `tests/sound/test_sound_state.cpp` = OLD `tests/test_sound_state.cpp` core vectors ported): the driven core (transfer, 3 voices, MVOL/BVOL, KON, handler, a 480-frame pump, a pending KOFF + a pending pitch) → **payload 2,104,512 bytes**, restore → identical re-serialise, the readbacks answer from the file's voices with zero holds, a perturbation changes the payload and restoring the original brings it back, the handler registry rides the section, **a fresh core restored from the payload mixes the next 800 frames bit-identically**; the validate guards (7 truncations, +1 byte, spu_size / voice_count corruption with named errors, a rejected restore leaves a good one intact); SDRX round trip across a clock jump (elapsed 100 frames, same `Status`); the `SectionRegistry` path (order SDRX, SNDC; both optional; `FileVersionFor == 4`; restore into a second registry → identical SNDC; an empty file restores). `all vectors passed`.
- **`SpuRegisterWindow`** (#114, `tests/sound/test_spu_register_window.cpp`): window membership incl. the KSEG1 alias, params through the window land on the libsd entry codes and read back (16/32/8-bit), ENVX/NAX read-only, SSA hi/lo → byte 0x24010, KON low half keys voice 3 (eager NAX visible as hi/lo), KON high half keys core-1 voice 18, ENDX read/clear, CORE ATTR, MVOLL core 0 / BVOLR core 1, KOFF applied by the pump, unmapped counted, `UnroutedCount()==0`, RAM path intact, window removal. `all vectors passed`.
- `MixerTickPump` ring vector rewritten for drop-oldest (entry 0 dropped, the Kon kept at the tail); `LibsdModule` SdrRemote vectors split into `Dispatch` (reference scope: `kInit` queues no SilenceAll, `kGetParam` → 0, `kSetCoreAttr` ignored) and `Invoke` (full model); the bindings vector `0x8020` → `$v0 = 0`.

### HANDOFF (4E‴ → 4F)
**Done:** the sound half is closed for Phase 4 — the realtime-policy safety (CmdRing + atomic `SpuCore`, `SpuCoreHammer`), the SdrRemote scope restored to OLD (decision recorded), the realtime transfer busy window recorded as an accepted difference, `stream_rpc_cmds=` in run.meta and P4-SND-BOOT-SHAPE pinned with the ids, `SoundStateCodec` (SNDC/SDRX, registered after TIMR, `SoundState` round trip incl. a bit-identical mix from a restored core), `IopMemory::SetBusWindow` + `SpuRegisterWindow` (`SpuRegisterWindow` test); both legs re-run and unchanged; full ctest green (quoted in the Gate).

**Not done / carried (in order):**
1. `FmvStream` + `BankLedger` (plan §2.7 rows) — Phase 7 feed; `SdBlockTrans` real mode then. `SoundStateCodec` gains the FMV FIFO fields with it (OLD's SNDC did not carry the FIFO either).
2. A per-command libsd census (which of `21/42/81/9/16/7/37` produces the `SdInit` group and which the per-core switch/addr walk) — a run.meta nicety for P4-SND-BOOT-SHAPE; not needed by the gate.
3. `core/diag/{padwatch, sndwatch}` DIAG categories — nothing in the deterministic legs needs them.
4. The 4E/4E′ carry list unchanged (`write` row park, `LoaderModel` producer, `latency_model`, `core/diag/loadspan`, the two workarounds, `archive_table`, BUG-040's reused-tid window, `PowerOffCB`, EQUIVALENCE §7 records when `mem.mhash` moves).

**The 4F question — proposal, not a decision.** The coldboot leg now parks on `libgraph.a sceGsResetGraph @0x2c1bd8`: the boot has finished the IOP/SIF/disc/mc/pad/sound init and is entering the GS init. The next rows on the path (`sceGsResetGraph`, `sceGsResetPath`, `sceGsSetDefDispEnv`, `sceGsPutDispEnv`, `sceGsSyncV`… — OLD `gs_hle.cpp`) are SDK rows whose BODIES write the GS privileged registers (PMODE/SMODE2/DISPFB/DISPLAY/CSR/IMR) and reset the GIF/VIF paths — Phase 6's owner (the GS) with Phase 5's paths (the `unhandled_mmio` pages `0x10003000/0x10003c00/0x10005000` are exactly GIF/VIF1/FIFO). Two ways to close Phase 4's gate:
- **(A) A libgraph HLE trap release in 4F**: land `engine/gs/hle/libgraph_bindings` as thin rows that write the privileged registers into the Phase-3 GS sink (`GsPrivRegs` already exists for the savestate GSPR section) and return the SDK's values, the way 4D″ pulled `sceDmaReset` forward (P4-LIBDMA-PARK). Pro: the boot reaches its first `Step::Verify` boundary inside Phase 4, so `snd_pumps`, `audio.ahash`, `mem.mhash` get their first real coldboot values and P4-SND-BOOT-SHAPE's heartbeat half (the per-boundary `SdGetParam`/`SdGetAddr` polls) becomes observable — the audio lane's first honest coldboot number. Con: it writes Phase-6 state from Phase 4 (the rows are trivially small, but `sceGsSyncV` is a vsync wait that only means something once the GS has a frame clock — the tick source's vblank already exists, so it is a `Wait::Vblank` park, not a stub); the Phase-6 owner then inherits rows it did not write.
- **(B) Stop Phase 4's gate at "the boot reaches the GS init"**: declare the coldboot leg's Phase-4 exit condition = `park_detail` is a libgraph/GS row with every earlier subsystem's census matching OLD (it does: the IOP reboot/module sequence, libcdvd, libmc, libpad, libsd/libsdr), and let Phase 5/6 own the release. Pro: clean ownership, no forward writes into GS state. Con: Phase 4 ends without a single coldboot boundary — the audio/mem lanes' coldboot baselines stay unclaimed until Phase 6, and the "sound closed" claim rests on the anchor legs + the unit vectors only (the anchor f46203 leg has `libsd_calls=` empty: it parks on VU micro before any sound traffic).
- **Proposal:** (A) for the four register-writing rows + a `Wait::Vblank` `sceGsSyncV`, scoped to "the boot reaches its first boundary", with each row recorded as "landed 4F, owner Phase 6" in the plan's §2 table the way the libdma rows were — because the phase gate that matters most for this stage's claims (the audio lane's first coldboot window, the heartbeat census) is one boundary away, and (B) would leave P4-SND-BOOT-SHAPE's second half unobservable for two more phases. If the reviewer prefers (B), the record needed is a one-line gate amendment in `CONSTRUCTION_PLAN.md` §4.4 ("coldboot: reaches the GS init with the IOP-side census OLD-equal") and nothing in code changes.

**Next command (4F opener):** `cmake --build --preset macos-dev -j4 && ctest --preset macos-dev -R 'SpuCoreHammer|SoundState|SpuRegisterWindow|LibsdModule|MixerTickPump|Lint'` (expect 8/8); then, per the decision above, either read `OLD/host_app/gs_hle.cpp` (`sceGsResetGraph`/`sceGsResetPath`/`sceGsSetDefDispEnv`/`sceGsPutDispEnv`/`sceGsSyncV` bodies) for (A) or amend §4.4 for (B); re-run `python3 verify/run_leg.py --binary build/app/host/nativeee --tree new --anchor coldboot --profile deterministic --frames 1500 --out /tmp/p4f/cb` and quote `park=`, `snd_pumps=`, `audio_first_nonsilent_window=`.

## 4F — the p4e4r items, the `--frames` cap for the EE path, the libgraph trap release (Option A: two rows + the `GsPrivRegs` shadow, owner Phase 6); **the coldboot leg reaches boundary 0 and runs 60 boundaries** — checkpoint 0 DIVERGENT from the baseline and NOT YET ATTRIBUTED; the 11 anchors restore and park on VU micro (Phase 5) (2026-09-28, ≈35 min; tree green at every stop)

### First 6 minutes — the p4e4r items
1. `SpuCore::RepublishReadbacks` (`engine/sound/spu_core.cpp`): OLD's consumer side restored exactly (`sound_core.cpp:834-838`) — a held voice is SKIPPED before the stores, the NAX/ENVX stores are seq_cst, the post-store recheck re-asserts the eager values.
2. `SpuCoreHammer`: the producer is PACED (one KON sweep over the 48 voices per batch, then it waits for the mixer to pump at least once), a pumps floor asserted (`kProducerIters / 48 = 416`), and every ENVX poll made while the voice's hold was up (hold read before AND after the poll) must be non-zero. Result: **`pumps=463 (floor 416) polls=40000 held_polls=19997 held_envx_zero=0 dropped=0 holds=0`** — the skip/recheck path ran against ~20 000 live holds and never published the watchdog's 0.
3. `SoundState`: the first restore now happens over a LIVE eager hold (SSA voice 5 ← 0xA000, KON voice 5 unapplied in the ring; the eager NAX 0xA000 / non-zero ENVX visible before): after `RestoreSndc` the hold is 0 and voice 5's NAX/ENVX answer from the FILE's voice (≠ 0xA000). `all vectors passed`.
4. run.meta gains **`iop_unrouted=`** (`IopMemory::UnroutedCount()`) and **`iop_bus_window_hits=`** (`BusWindowHits()`) — coldboot: `iop_unrouted=0 iop_bus_window_hits=0` (the SPU2 window is served by `SpuCore` through libsd; the recompiled module does not touch `0x1F900000` directly on this path).
5. P4-SND-REALTIME-SAFE (plan §7.1) gained the drop-oldest sentence: a dropped KON leaves its eager hold up until the next `SilenceAll`, so the voice keeps publishing the eager values (OLD `:842-848`); the hammer's `holds == 0` is conditioned on `Dropped() == 0`.

### The attribution method (recorded; the reviewer's, now the tree's — plan row P4-CKPT0-METHOD)
The reviewer (p4fr) stopped both binaries under `lldb` at the boundary-0 hash and dumped RAM/SPAD/arena (`/tmp/p4fr/{old,new}_{ram,spad,arena}.bin`, 32 MiB / 16 KiB / 2 MiB). 4F′ verified the dumps ARE the boundary-0 images: OLD's, wrapped as a synthetic `state_b0.ps2st` (`/tmp/p4f2/residual.py`), hashes to **`0xeec37d056857657b` = the frozen baseline's `mem.mhash` line 0**; NEW's new `--state-every` drop `state_b0.ps2st` hashes (`verify/state_hash.py`) to exactly its `mem.mhash` line 0 (`0x7b92ccfb3755a5b1` both). So `mem.mhash` = FNV-1a64 over RAM0 → SPAD → META in `state_hash.py`'s chain on BOTH trees, and a boundary-N image is comparable page by page. Class attribution = a 4 KiB page diff of the two images with the ELF `.text` zeroed (`/tmp/p4f2/pagediff.py`, `residual.py`).

### Adjudication (the RETAIL rule decides; one §7.1 row per class in `PHASE4_PLAN.md`)

## 4F″ — the SpuCoreHammer race root-caused and fixed at the engine layer (one 64-bit readback word, CAS on both sides; 30/30 under `ctest -j8` load); the open checkpoint-0 classes re-examined with the images (values named; `0x2ea850` writer found); the loader seams NOT bound (park unchanged at frame 60) (2026-09-28, 11:17–11:52 work / docs to ≈12:00; tree green at every stop)

### First 13 minutes — the intermittent failure (`test_spu_core_hammer.cpp:148: held_envx_zero.load() == 0`)
**Diagnosis: an ENGINE ordering bug, not a test-side observation race.** The pump's consumer side (4F) was `load hold → if 0: store NAX, store ENVX → recheck hold → re-store eager`. The check and the stores are separate atomic operations, so a KON that lands between them has its eager ENVX overwritten by the stale pre-KON model (ENVX 0) until the recheck repairs it — and an EE-side poll inside that window reads ENVX 0 with the hold up. The proving interleaving (voice `vi` silent, model `Envx()==0`; the test's producer thread is both the KON issuer and the poller, exactly like the EE thread):

```
producer (EE side)                              mixer (RepublishReadbacks, voice vi)
                                                C1  eager_kon[vi].load()             -> 0      (no hold yet)
                                                --- preempted (the ctest -j8 load makes this likely) ---
P1  eager_kon[vi].fetch_add(1)                  -> hold = 1
P2  nax[vi].store(SSA)
P3  envx[vi].store(0x1FFF)                      eager attack step (ADSR1 rate 0)
P4  hold_before = EagerHold(vi)                 -> 1
                                                C2  nax[vi].store(model Nax())
                                                C3  envx[vi].store(model Envx())      -> 0      STALE, hold is up
P5  envx = GetParam(ENVX)                       -> 0   <-- the watchdog-abort value
P6  hold_after = EagerHold(vi)                  -> 1   (the Kon is still in the ring; only ApplyCommand(Kon) lowers it)
                                                C4  recheck eager_kon[vi].load()       -> 1 -> re-store 0x1FFF  (too late: P5 saw 0)
```
The test DOES re-read the hold after ENVX (`hold_after`) and it is still 1, so a test-side "re-check the hold" fix cannot filter it — the observation is legitimate: between C3 and C4 the published word is `{hold=1, ENVX=0}`, which the eager-hold protocol exists to forbid. Publishing the eager values BEFORE raising the hold (the other candidate fix) does not close it either: the stale store C3 comes after both regardless of the producer's order. Only atomicity of "check the hold and publish" closes it.

**Fix (engine layer, `engine/sound/spu_core.{h,cpp}`):** NAX (low 32), ENVX (bits 32–47) and the key-on hold count (bits 48–63, saturating) of a voice are ONE `std::atomic<uint64_t> readback_[vi]`. `PublishKeyOn` raises the hold and publishes the eager NAX/ENVX in a single CAS; `RepublishReadbacks` publishes the model with `compare_exchange_strong(cur → {0, Envx, Nax})` taken only when `cur` shows hold 0 — a KON landing after the load changes the hold field and the CAS fails, the eager values stay; `ReleaseKeyOnHold` (on Kon apply) and `ClearEagerHolds` (SilenceAll / savestate) are CAS loops that keep the published values. No ABA: the pump CASes only from hold 0, and hold 0 is left only by the pump's own release. `GetParam(ENVX)`/`GetAddr(NAX)`/`EagerHold` unpack the word. The separate `nax_`/`envx_`/`eager_kon_` arrays and the post-store recheck are gone; the header comment states the rule. The savestate codec's `ClearEagerHolds()`/`RepublishReadbacks()` calls are unchanged.

**Proof:** isolated `held_polls=19998 held_envx_zero=0`; **30/30 bounded hammer runs under a looping `ctest --preset macos-dev -j8` (`/tmp/p4f3/hammer30.sh`, `/tmp/p4f3/hammer30.log`: `hammer30: pass=30 fail=0`, `held_envx_zero=0` in every run, pumps 1033–1370 per run against the 416 floor)**; then the full suite clean: **`100% tests passed, 0 tests failed out of 115`** (`/tmp/p4f3/ctest_full.log`, `LintOneClass`/`LintEnginePurity` inside). Row P4-SND-REALTIME-SAFE amended.

### The VU op that parks the boot (item 3)
`park_pc=0x204020` is STALE — `ctx.cpuRegs.pc` is written only when an HLE row returns (`sdk_binding_manifest.cpp:185`, `pc = $ra`), so it names the return address of the last served row (`FUN_002c71bc`, a libc memset-class row called from `0x204014`), not the parking site. The site, from `lldb` on the NEW binary (`/tmp/p4f5/lldb_vu.log`, breakpoint on `Vu0Macro::Execute`): **`Vu0Macro::Execute(…, start_addr=240)` from `FUN_0018b918` (`recompiled_shard_009.cpp:8845`) — the title's `vcallms 0x1e` (VU0 micro byte address `0xf0`)**. The routine is the title's angle→sin/cos: `cvt.s.w f0 ← [a0]`, `f0 *= 0x38c90fdb` (2π/65536), `swc1` to the frame, `lqc2 vf20 ← frame`, `vcallms 0xf0`, `sqc2 vf31 → frame`, `lwc1` back — VU0 micro at `0xf0` maps vf20 → vf31 (the sin/cos micro subroutine; 54 `vcallms 0xf0` sites in the shards, the most-used of the 16 distinct VU0 micro entries). `park_detail` now carries it: **`park_detail=vcallms micro pc 0xf0 (VU micro memory + interpreter absent)`** (`seams.cpp` `VuParkDetail`; a CMSAR1 start reports `cmsar1 start micro pc 0x…`). Corroboration: `dmac_unmodelled_kicks=1:0x1` — the ONE unmodelled DMA kick of the boot is channel 0 (VIF0): the title's MPG upload of the VU0 microprogram, which NEW counts and does not move. **Phase 5 must land, for the boot to pass frame 60:** DMAC ch0 → VIF0 → `MPG` into VU0 micro memory (the microprogram carrying the `0xf0` subroutine), then `Vu0Macro::Execute` = the VU0 micro interpreter from `start_addr` to the E-bit (VF20 in, VF31 out, Q/flags), with the M-bit interlock (`ResumeIfWaiting`). An interim INERT `vcallms` is NOT legitimate: the result vector is consumed by the caller (`lwc1` of the frame word), so the boot would compute with garbage.

### The ULP class settled (item 4) — P4-EE-ROUNDING (NEW was wrong; fixed)
The DIV/SQRT/RSQRT helpers are identical in the two trees (OLD `cpu_state.h:31-54` ≡ NEW `ps2_float.inl`: clamp, `FE_TONEAREST` swapped in, single precision, clamp) — so the helpers were not the operation. **The difference is the AMBIENT rounding mode:** OLD sets `fesetround(FE_TOWARDZERO)` on every thread that runs recompiled EE code (`main.cpp:694` `[R24 EE-CHOP]`, `ps2_threading.cpp:466` `ps2WorkerMain`), so MUL/ADD/SUB/CVT round toward zero (PCSX2's default EE FPU round mode = Chop/Zero, no GameDB override for SLUS-12345); NEW declared `Ps2Float::SetGuestThreadRounding()` (`ps2_float.h:22`, the same rule in its comment) but **NOTHING CALLED IT** — NEW's guest threads ran at the host default `FE_TONEAREST`. That is exactly the signature recorded in 4F″/4F‴: NEW's 1/√17 tables were the correctly-ROUNDED `…42`/`…44` while OLD's were the TRUNCATED `…43`; and the `0x1fff`/`0x2000` statics are a `cvt.w.s` of a product just under 8192 (truncation → `0x1fff`, nearest → `0x2000`). **Fix (2 sites):** `ThreadManager::RunGuestThread` and the composition root (`ee_bootstrap.cpp`, before `DispatchTable::Initialize` — that thread runs recompiled code too, `lldb` shows `FUN_0018b918` on `ps2r-main`) call `Ps2Float::SetGuestThreadRounding()`. **Verdict (a): OLD's mode is the PS2-exact one (the EE FPU truncates — PCSX2 Chop/Zero), NEW fixed;** proof = the four ULP regions (61 words) and the nine statics are byte-identical to OLD at boundary 0 after the fix, nothing else moved (the 7 remaining pages are the same words as before). PCSX2 was not consulted (no `pcsx2_status` attempt this stage — the OLD-identical result under the documented PCSX2 default is the proof; a PCSX2 read of `0x3b4a64` remains a cheap confirmation for Phase 5's opener). Standing requirement alongside P4-FPCONTRACT: every host thread that executes recompiled EE/VU0-macro code runs under `FE_TOWARDZERO`; the helpers swap `FE_TONEAREST` for DIV/SQRT/RSQRT only.
